"""

# Extract all patterns in the lookbehinds
# Each lookbehind starts with (?<=.{N})(?<!.{N+1})(\.*PATTERN\.*)
# Let's use regex to find these segments.

pattern = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
matches = re.findall(pattern, script_content)

segments = []
for m in matches:
    start_index = int(m[0])
    end_index = int(m[1]) # This should be start_index + 1
    pattern_str = m[2]
    segments.append((start_index, pattern_str))

for i, (start, p) in enumerate(segments):
    print(f"Segment {i} (starts at {start}): {p}")

"""

def convert_pattern(p):
    # Replace #{n} with n hashes
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    # Replace \.+ with one dot
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    # Replace \. with one dot
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    return p

matches = []
# Re-run extraction but more carefully
pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

for start, end, content in found:
    matches.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

for i, (start, p) in enumerate(matches):
    print(f"Segment {i} (starts at {start}): {p}")
    print(f"Length of converted pattern: {len(p)}")

"""

def convert_pattern(p):
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    return p

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

segments = []
for start, end, content in found:
    segments.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

# Let's assume each segment is 101 characters.
# The pattern describes the core. We'll pad it with dots at the beginning.
# And each segment ends with a dot.
rows = []
for start, core in segments:
    # The core is what's between \.* and \.*
    # But in my regex, I'm getting the core part.
    # Let's try to pad the core to 100 characters.
    # The last character of the segment is a dot (at index start + 100).
    padding_len = 100 - len(core)
    if padding_len < 0:
        # This means our core is too long, maybe the \.+ was actually one or more dots.
        # For now, let's just take the first 100 characters of the core.
        row = core[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core + "."
    rows.append(row)

# We are missing the first row (0-100). Let's see if we can find it.
# The segments found are: 101, 202, ..., 1818.
# These are rows 1 to 18 (if row 0 is 0-100).
# Wait, that's only 18 rows. We need 19.
# Let's see if we can find the 19th row.
# The last segment is at 1818, which is row 18.
# So rows are 0, 1, ..., 18. Total 19.

# Let's try to print the rows as a grid.
for r in rows:
    print(r)

#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

def convert_pattern(p):
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    return p

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

segments = []
for start, end, content in found:
    segments.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

rows = []
for start, core in segments:
    # core is like ##.+#.+##...
    # Replace .+ with .
    core_cleaned = re.sub(r'\.+', '.', core)
    # Pad the core to 100 characters
    padding_len = 100 - len(core_cleaned)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core_cleaned[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core_cleaned + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    # Replace # with # and . with space
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

def convert_pattern(p):
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    return p

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

segments = []
for start, end, content in found:
    segments.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

rows = []
for start, core in segments:
    # core is like ##.+#.+##...
    # Replace .+ with .
    core_cleaned = re.sub(r'\.+', '.', core)
    # Pad the core to 100 characters
    padding_len = 100 - len(core_cleaned)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core_cleaned[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core_cleaned + "."
    rows.append(row)

# Let's look at the rows more closely.
for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

def convert_pattern(p):
    # The input 'p' is a string from a regex, so we need to handle escaped dots.
    # For example, '\\.' in the string is a literal dot in the regex.
    # So we want to replace '\\.' with '.' and '\\\.+' with '.'.
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    return p

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

segments = []
for start, end, content in found:
    segments.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

rows = []
for start, core in segments:
    # The core is now a string of # and .
    # We assume the segment is 101 characters.
    # The core is the content, and it might be padded with dots.
    # Let's just use the core and see.
    rows.append(core)

for r in rows:
    # Print with '#' and ' ' (for .)
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

"""

import re

def convert_pattern(p):
    # Replace the literal sequence \. followed by one or more +
    # or just \.
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    return p

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

segments = []
for start, end, content in found:
    segments.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

rows = []
for start, core in segments:
    # Let's remove all + characters, since we've already handled \.+
    core_no_plus = core.replace('+', '')
    # Pad to 100
    padding_len = 100 - len(core_no_plus)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core_no_plus[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core_no_plus + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

"""

import re

def convert_pattern(p):
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    return p

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

segments = []
for start, end, content in found:
    segments.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

rows = []
for start, core in segments:
    core_no_plus = core.replace('+', '')
    padding_len = 100 - len(core_no_plus)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core_no_plus[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core_no_plus + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

def convert_pattern(p):
    # Replace the literal sequence \. followed by one or more +
    # or just \.
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', p)
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), p)
    return p

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

segments = []
for start, end, content in found:
    segments.append((int(start), convert_pattern(content)))

rows = []
for start, core in segments:
    # core is like ##.+#.+##...
    # Replace all non-hash/non-dot characters with space
    core_cleaned = ""
    for char in core:
        if char == '#':
            core_cleaned += '#'
        elif char == '.':
            core_cleaned += '.'
        else:
            core_cleaned += ' '
    
    # Pad to 100
    padding_len = 100 - len(core_cleaned)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core_cleaned[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core_cleaned + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

import re

def convert_pattern(p):
    # Handle the literal \. and \.+
    p = re.sub(r'\\\. \+', '.', p) # Wait, this is wrong. Let me try something else.
    pass

# Let's use a simpler way.
# Every character in the core is either '#', '.', '\', '+'.
# We want to replace '\.' with '.' and '\.+' with '.' and then remove '+'.
# Actually, let's just replace '\.' with '.' and then remove all '+'.
def clean_core(core):
    core = core.replace('\\.', '.')
    core = core.replace('+', '')
    return core

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

rows = []
for start, end, content in found:
    core = clean_core(content)
    # Pad to 100
    padding_len = 100 - len(core)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

"""

import re

def clean_core(core):
    # Replace #{n} with n hashes
    core = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), core)
    # Replace \.+ with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', core)
    # Replace \. with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', core)
    # Remove any remaining +
    core = core.replace('+', '')
    return core

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

rows = []
for start, end, content in found:
    core = clean_core(content)
    padding_len = 100 - len(core)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

"""

import re

def clean_core(core):
    # Replace #{n} with n hashes
    core = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), core)
    # Replace \.+ with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', core)
    # Replace \. with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', core)
    # Remove any remaining +
    core = core.replace('+', '')
    return core

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

rows = []
for start, end, content in found:
    core = clean_core(content)
    # Pad to 100
    padding_len = 100 - len(core)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

"""

import re

def clean_core(core):
    # Replace #{n} with n hashes
    core = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), core)
    # Replace \.+ with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', core)
    # Replace \. with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', core)
    # Remove any remaining +
    core = core.replace('+', '')
    return core

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

rows = []
for start, end, content in found:
    core = clean_core(content)
    padding_len = 100 - len(core)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

import re

def clean_core(core):
    # Replace #{n} with n hashes
    core = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', lambda m: '#' * int(m.group(1)), core)
    # Replace \.+ with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.+', '.', core)
    # Replace \. with .
    core = re.sub(r'\\\.', '.', core)
    # Remove any remaining +
    core = core.replace('+', '')
    return core

pattern_extraction = r"\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\(\\\.\*(.*?)\\\.\*\)"
found = re.findall(pattern_extraction, script_content)

rows = []
for start, end, content in found:
    core = clean_core(content)
    padding_len = 100 - len(core)
    if padding_len < 0:
        row = core[:100] + "."
    else:
        row = "." * padding_len + core + "."
    rows.append(row)

for r in rows:
    # Replace # with # and . with space
    print(r.replace('#', '#').replace('.', ' '))

```bash
$ [python] import re

pattern1 = r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)"

# We want to see how many # and . are in the pattern.
# However, the pattern uses + and *, so the number of dots can vary.
# But the total length of the row is 101.
# Let's assume the pattern describes a unique string.
# If we replace all \.* and \.+ with a single . and all # with #, 
# we can see the minimum number of characters.

def count_min_chars(p):
    # Replace all \.+ with . and \.* with nothing
    p = p.replace(r'\.+', '.')
    p = p.replace(r'\.*', '')
    # Now count the length of the string of . and #
    # But we need to be careful with the regex syntax.
    # A simpler way: the total number of # is fixed.
    # Let's count the # in the regex.
    num_hashes = len(re.findall(r'#+', p))
    # Wait, that's not right.
    # Let's use a different approach.
    
    # The number of # is the sum of all the numbers in the #{n} and the single #
    # Let's extract all the counts of #.
    hash_counts = []
    # This is a bit hacky.
    for match in re.finditer(r'#{(\d+)}|#', p):
        if match.group(1):
            hash_counts.append(int(match.group(1)))
        else:
            hash_counts.append(1)
    return sum(hash_counts)

print(f"Min hashes in pattern1: {count_min_chars(pattern1)}")

#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/"""

# We'll use a different method to find all lookbehinds.
# Each lookbehind starts with (?<=.{101*n})(?<!.{101*n+1})(\(.*?\))
# We can use a regex to find these.

pattern_regex = r'\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\((\.*?)\)'
# Wait, the lookbehinds in the string are actually like (?<=.{101})(?<!.{102})(\(.*?\))
# Let's use a loop to find them.

results = []
for i in range(1, 19):
    # Construction of the lookbehind pattern for row i
    # Row 1 is at 101, Row 2 is at 202, ... Row 18 is at 1818
    start_pos = 101 * i
    end_pos = start_pos + 1
    lookbehind = f"(?<=.{start_pos})(?<!.{end_pos})"
    # We want to find the pattern following this lookbehind.
    # The pattern is in parentheses.
    # Let's search for it.
    
    # Actually, it's easier to just use the string directly and find the patterns.
    # Let's find the position of "(?<=.{101})(?<!.{102})" and then find the next "(" and ")"
    pass

# Let's just try searching for the literal strings in regex_str.
for i in range(1, 19):
    lb = f"(?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})"
    # We want to find where this occurs in the regex_str.
    # But the regex_str itself contains these.
    # We can use string.find() to find the position of the lookbehind.
    # However, the lookbehind is not a literal string, it has variable length in the regex.
    # But we know it's exactly like "(?<=.{101})(?<!.{102})".
    lb_literal = f"(?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})"
    idx = regex_str.find(lb_literal)
    if idx != -1:
        # The pattern starts after the lookbehind.
        # The lookbehind is 21 characters long: "(?<=.{101})(?<!.{102})"
        # Wait, let's count. "(?<=.{101})(?<!.{102})"
        # "(?<=.{101})" is 11 chars. "(?<!.{102})" is 11 chars. Total 22.
        # Let's find the next "(" and the corresponding ")"
        start_pattern = idx + len(lb_literal)
        # Now find the pattern. It should be ( ... )
        # We need to find the first '(' and then the matching ')'
        paren_start = regex_str.find('(', start_pattern)
        # Now find the matching ')'
        # Since there are no nested parentheses in the patterns (they are simple),
        # we can just find the first ')'.
        paren_end = regex_str.find(')', paren_start)
        pattern = regex_str[paren_start+1:paren_end]
        results.append(pattern)

print(results)

for i in range(1, 19):
    lb = f"(?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})"
    idx = regex_str.find(lb)
    if idx != -1:
        start_pattern = idx + len(lb)
        paren_start = regex_str.find('(', start_pattern)
        paren_end = regex_str.find(')', paren_start)
        pattern = regex_str[paren_start+1:paren_end]
        print(f"Row {i}: {pattern}")

# So I need to match (?<=.{101})(?<!.{102})(\(.*?\))
# Let's use:
find_patterns = re.findall(r'\(\?<=.{(\d+)}\)\(\?<!.{(\d+)}\)\((.*?)\)', regex_str)
# This won't work because (?<=...) is not a group.
# It's: (?<=.{101})(?<!.{102})(...)
# Let's search for it.
pattern_list = []
for i in range(1, 19):
    search_str = f"(?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})"
    idx = regex_str.find(search_str)
    if idx != -1:
        # Find the start of the next group
        # The lookbehind ends at idx + len(search_str)
        start_group = regex_str.find('(', idx + len(search_str))
        if start_group != -1:
            # Find the end of the group
            # Since they are not nested, the next ')' is the end.
            end_group = regex_str.find(')', start_group)
            pattern_list.append(regex_str[start_group+1:end_group])

print(pattern_list)

import re

for i in range(1, 19):
    search_str = f"(?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})"
    idx = regex_str.find(search_str)
    if idx != -1:
        start_group = regex_str.find('(', idx + len(search_str))
        if start_group != -1:
            end_group = regex_str.find(')', start_group)
            pattern = regex_str[start_group+1:end_group]
            print(f"Row {i}: {pattern}")
    else:
        print(f"Row {i}: NOT FOUND (search_str: {search_str})")

import re

for i in range(1, 19):
    search_str = f"(?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})"
    idx = regex_str.find(search_str)
    if idx != -1:
        start_group = regex_str.find('(', idx + len(search_str))
        if start_group != -1:
            end_group = regex_str.find(')', start_group)
            pattern = regex_str[start_group+1:end_group]
            print(f"Row {i}: {pattern}")
    else:
        print(f"Row {i}: NOT FOUND (search_str: {search_str})")

import re

for i in range(1, 19):
    search_str = f"(?<=.{{101*i}})(?<!.{{101*i+1}})"
    idx = regex_str.find(search_str)
    if idx != -1:
        start_group = regex_str.find('(', idx + len(search_str))
        if start_group != -1:
            end_group = regex_str.find(')', start_group)
            pattern = regex_str[start_group+1:end_group]
            print(f"Row {i}: {pattern}")
    else:
        print(f"Row {i}: NOT FOUND (search_str: {search_str})")

for i in range(1, 19):
    search_str = f"(?<=.{{{101*i}}})(?<!.{{{101*i+1}}})"
    idx = regex_str.find(search_str)
    if idx != -1:
        start_group = regex_str.find('(', idx + len(search_str))
        if start_group != -1:
            end_group = regex_str.find(')', start_group)
            pattern = regex_str[start_group+1:end_group]
            print(f"Row {i}: {pattern}")
    else:
        print(f"Row {i}: NOT FOUND (search_str: {search_str})")

# Extract all the column indices mentioned in the lookaheads
# Lookaheads look like (?=(?:.{A}\..{B})*...)
# where A is the column index and A+1+B = 101.
# Let's find all patterns like (?:.{NUMBER}\..{NUMBER})
pattern = re.compile(r'\{\.(?P<A>\d+)\}\.\{(?P<B>\d+)\}') # This is wrong, it's not using {}.
# It's using .{A}
pattern = re.compile(r'\.\{(?P<A>\d+)\}') # No, it's .{\d+}
# Let's just search for the pattern .{\d+}
# Actually, it's .{A} where A is the number.
# Let's use a more direct approach.
# The lookahead is (?=(?:.{A}\..{B})*...)
# We can search for the substring ".{X}\..{Y}"
pattern = re.compile(r'\.\{(?P<X>\d+)\}\.\{(?P<Y>\d+)\}') # Still wrong.
# The regex in js_code is:
# (?:.{91}\..{9})
# So I'll search for \.\{(?P<X>\d+)\}\.\{(?P<Y>\d+)\}
# Wait, the regex is .{91}\..{9}
# So it's dot, then {91}, then \., then then dot, then {9},
# Actually, it's \. and then {9} is not there, it's . {9}.
# Let's use:
pattern = re.compile(r'\.\{(?P<X>\d+)\}\.\{(?P<Y>\d+)\}') # No.
# Let's look at the js_code again.
# it's .{91}\..{9}
# Ah, it's . followed by {91}, then \. (literal dot), then . followed by {9}.
# So it's \.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\} --- No.
# It's \.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\}
# Wait, in the js_code, it's .{91}\..{9}
# This means it's . then {91}, then \. then . then {9}
# So the pattern is: \.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\} --- wait, no.
# It's \.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\} in my search, but in the string it is:
# . { 9 1 } \ . . { 9 }
# So it's \.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\} --- No.
# It's \.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\} --- Let's just use a simple search.

import re
# Let's find all patterns like .{NUMBER}\..{NUMBER}
matches = re.findall(r'\.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\}', js_code)
# Wait, the js_code has .{91}\..{9}
# So it is dot, brace, 9, 1, brace, backslash, dot, dot, brace, 9, brace.
# No, it's . { 9 1 } \ . . { 9 }
# Let me re-read.
# It's .{91}\..{9}
# In Python, this would be r'\.\{(\d+)\}\.\.\{(\d+)\}' if I'm not careful.
# But in the string, it is literally: dot, brace, 9, 1, brace, backslash, dot, dot, brace, 9, brace.
# Let me use a regex to find this.
pattern = r'\.\{(\d+)\}\\\.\.\{(\d+)\}' # No.
# Let's use:
pattern = r'\.\{(\d+)\}\\\.\.\{(\d+)\}'
# Let's try again.
import re
matches = re.findall(r'\.\{(\d+)\}\\\.\.\{(\d+)\}', js_code)
print(matches)

{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1616})(?<!.{1617})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

# Extract all lookaheads
# Lookaheads are in the form (?=(?:.{A}\..{B})*(?:.{A}#.{B}){N}(?:.{A}\..{B})+(?:.{A}#.{B}){M}(?:.{A}\..{B})*)
# or (?=(?:.{A}\..{B}){19})

# etc.

# Let's use a regex to find all these lookaheads.
# The lookaheads are inside (?=(...))
lookahead_pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?=(.*?)\)')
lookaheads = lookahead_pattern.findall(script_js)

print(f"Found {len(lookaheads)} lookaheads.")

# Now let's parse each lookahead.
# A lookahead can be:
# 1. (?:.{A}\..{B})* (?:.{A}#.{B}){N} (?:.{A}\..{B})+ (?:.{A}#.{B}){M} (?:.{A}\..{B})*
# 2. (?:.{A}\..{B}){19}
# 3. (?:.{A}\..{B})* (?:.{A}#.{B}){N} (?:.{A}\..{B})+ (?:.{A}#.{B}){M} (?:.{A}\..{B})+(?:.{A}#.{B}){P} (?:.{A}\..{B})*

# This is quite complex. Let's try to extract A and the pattern of '#' and '.' for each block.
# For a given A, a block's character at index A is determined by the sequence of segments.

def parse_lookahead(la):
    # Each segment is like (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # We want to find the A and the type (. or #)
    # Let's find all instances of .{A} followed by \. or #
    # The A is the number in .{A}
    
    # We can use re.findall to find all segments
    # Segment format: (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # Let's find them.
    segments = re.findall(r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].{?(\d+)?}\)', la)
    # Wait, the regex is like (?:.{91}\..{9})
    # My regex might be wrong. Let's refine.
    return segments

# Let's try a different approach. Let's find all occurrences of .{A}#.{B} and .{A}\..{B}
# In the lookahead.
for i, la in enumerate(lookaheads):
    # la is the content inside (?=...)
    # We want to find A and whether it's # or .
    # A segment is either (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # We'll find all such segments and their repetitions.
    
    # Let's use a simpler regex to find segments.
    # A segment is (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # Let's look for the patterns:
    # .{A}\..{B}
    # .{A}#.{B}
    
    # We need to handle the repetitions like (?:...)*, (?:...){N}, (?:...)+
    # This is a bit like parsing a formal grammar.
    pass

# Let's try to extract A and whether it's # or . for each segment in each lookahead.
# A segment is (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
# We can find them with:
segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].{?(\d+)?}\)')
# Wait, the B can be optional.
# Let's try again.
for i, la in enumerate(lookaheads):
    # We'll find all occurrences of the pattern
    # We'll also find the repetition: *, +, {N}
    
    # Let's look for patterns like:
    # (?:.{A}\..{B})
    # (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # and then look for the multiplier after the closing parenthesis.
    
    # First, find all segments:
    # We'll look for (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # We'll use a regex that captures the A, the type (# or .), and the B.
    # Then we'll look for the multiplier.
    
    # The segment itself:
    # (?:.{A}\..{B})  => groups: (A, '.', B)
    # (?:.{A}#.{B})   => groups: (A, '#', B)
    
    # Let's find the segments and their multipliers.
    # This is hard with just regex.
    pass

# Let's try a different way. 
# Every lookahead is a sequence of segments.
# Each segment is either (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B}).
# The segments are separated by their multipliers.
# Let's find all (?:.{A}[.#].{B}) and the multipliers.

def get_segments(la):
    # A segment is (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # We'll find all occurrences of this and then check for the multiplier.
    # The multiplier can be *, +, or {N}.
    
    segments = []
    # Find all (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # Use a regex to find the segments.
    # Note: B can be empty or omitted in some cases? No, looking at the script, B is always there.
    # Wait, look at (?:.{100}\.){19}. This is different.
    # It's (?:.{100}\.){19}. A=100, type=., B=0.
    
    # Let's look for the segments in the lookahead.
    # They look like: (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # or (?:.{A}\.) or (?:.{A}\#)
    
    # Let's use a more robust way to find segments.
    # A segment starts with (?: and ends with )
    # Inside, it's .{A}[.#].{B}
    
    # We'll use a regex to find all (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # And we'll also find the multipliers.
    
    # Let's try this:
    pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].{?(\d+)?}\)')
    # This doesn't quite work because the B is inside the curly braces.
    # Let's try:
    pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)')
    # This is also not quite right.
    
    # Let's just find all segments and their multipliers.
    # A segment is (?: ... )
    # We can find all (?: ... ) in the la.
    
    # Let's use a regex to find all (?: ...)
    # and then parse the inside.
    
    segment_matches = re.finditer(r'\(\?:\.(?P<type>[.#]).*?\)', la)
    # Wait, the segment is (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # Let's try:
    # (?:.{A}\..{B})
    # (?:.{A}#.{B})
    
    # Let's look for the content of each (?:...)
    # The content is .{A}[.#].{B}
    
    # Let's try:
    matches = re.finditer(r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)', la)
    # Still not quite right.
    
    # Let's just use a very simple approach: 
    # Find all instances of .{A}#.{B} and .{A}\..{B} in the lookahead.
    # And also find the multiplicity.
    
    # A segment is a pattern like .{A}#.{B} or .{A}\..{B}
    # It is inside (?:...)
    
    # Let's find all (?:...)
    # And for each, we'll extract A, the type, and the multiplier.
    
    # We'll find all (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # The A is (\d+), the type is [.#], and the B is (\d+)
    
    # Let's use a regex to find the whole segment:
    # \(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)
    # This is still tricky because of the B.
    
    # Let's try:
    # \(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)
    # Wait, the B is inside {}
    # So it's like .{91}\..{9}
    # Let's find segments.
    
    import re
    
    # Regex for: (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # We can use: \(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)
    # Let's try to find the segments and then see what follows them.
    
    segment_finder = re.finditer(r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)', la)
    for match in segment_finder:
        A = int(match.group(1))
        content = match.group(0)
        # The type is . or #
        # We can find it in content.
        if '\\.' in content:
            t = '.'
        else:
            t = '#'
        
        # Find the multiplier after the segment
        # We'll look at the text immediately following the match.
        end_pos = match.end()
        # The multiplier can be *, +, or {N}
        multiplier = ""
        if end_pos < len(la):
            if la[end_pos] == '*':
                multiplier = '*'
            elif la[end_pos] == '+':
                multiplier = '+'
            elif la[end_pos] == '{':
                m = re.match(r'\{(\d+)\}', la[end_pos:])
                if m:
                    multiplier = m.group(1)
        
        segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': multiplier})
    return segments

for i, la in enumerate(lookaheads):
    segs = get_segments(la)
    print(f"Lookahead {i}: {segs}")

def get_segments(la):
    segments = []
    # A segment is (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # We find all (?:...) and then parse the inside.
    # The inside is .{A}[.#].{B}
    # Let's use a regex to find all (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # The segments are like (?:.{91}\..{9}) or (?:.{91}#.{9})
    # We'll use a regex to find these.
    
    # We need to handle the case where B is empty or something.
    # The segment is (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # Let's find all (?:...)
    segment_matches = re.finditer(r'\(\?:\.(?P<type>[.#]).*?\}\)', la)
    # This is still not quite right. Let's use a regex to find the pattern.
    # The pattern is .{A}[.#].{B}
    # We want to find all (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    
    # Let's use a regex to find the (?:...) part.
    # Then we'll check if it matches the pattern.
    
    # Let's try to find all occurrences of .{A}[.#].{B} inside (?:...)
    # Note: B is in {B}.
    
    # Let's try finding all (?:...) and then parse them.
    # The content of (?:...) is .{A}[.#].{B}
    
    # A simpler way:
    # The lookahead is a sequence of segments.
    # Let's split the lookahead into segments.
    # Each segment is (?:.{A}[.#].{B}) followed by a multiplier.
    
    # Let's use a regex that finds the (?:...) and its multiplier.
    # The segment itself can be (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    # The pattern inside (?:...) is .{A}[.#].{B}
    
    # Let's use re.finditer to find all (?:...)
    # Then for each, we find the multiplier.
    
    # The segment pattern:
    # \(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)
    
    # Let's try to find the segments.
    # This is the pattern for a segment: (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # Let's use a regex to find this.
    # Note: \. is a literal dot.
    
    # We can find segments by looking for the pattern:
    # (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
    
    # Let's try:
    # pattern = r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)'
    # This should work.
    
    segment_finder = re.finditer(r'\(\?:\.{(\d+)}[\.#].*?\}\)', la)
    for match in segment_finder:
        A = int(match.group(1))
        content = match.group(0)
        t = '.' if '\\.' in content else '#'
        
        # Now find the multiplier immediately after the match
        end_pos = match.end()
        multiplier = ""
        if end_pos < len(la):
            if la[end_pos] == '*':
                multiplier = '*'
            elif la[end_pos] == '+':
                multiplier = '+'
            elif la[end_pos] == '{':
                m = re.match(r'\{(\d+)\}', la[end_pos:])
                if m:
                    multiplier = m.group(1)
        
        segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': multiplier})
    return segments

# Let's just use the lookbehinds to fill the grid.
# The lookbehinds are very specific.
# They describe each block.
# For each block, the pattern is (dots, pattern, dots).
# The pattern itself is (dots, #, dots, #, ...).
# Since there are so many lookaheads, the positions of # must be fixed.

# Let's try to see if we can just "solve" the grid by

# Let's extract the lookbehind patterns.
# Block 1: (\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)
# Let's convert this into a list of (#, count) and (., count).
# But the . is .+, which means 1 or more.

#   (3, #)

#   (1, #)
#   (1, .)
#   (3, #)
#   (dots, any)

    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*)",
]

def parse_pattern(p):
    # p is something like (\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}...)
    # Let's replace \. with . and # with # and \.+ with .+
    # and then find all # and .
    # But we need to know the number of # and .
    p = p[1:-1] # remove (\ and *)
    # The pattern is: \.* (dots), # (hash), \.+ (dots)
    # Let's replace:
    # \.* with (DOTS, 0)
    # # with (#, 1)
    # {N} with (char, N)
    # \.+ with (DOTS, 1)
    # \. with (., 1)
    
    # Let's use a more systematic approach.
    # We'll use regex to find all elements.
    # Elements: \.*, #, \.+, \#{N}, \.{N}, etc.
    
    # Actually, let's just find all sequences of # and .
    # A # is #, and a . is \.
    # A # is also #{N}.
    # A . is also \.{N} or \.+
    
    # Let's use a regex to find all # and . in the pattern.
    # This is a bit tricky because of the \.
    
    # Let's replace:
    # \#\{(\d+)\} -> #, group(1)
    # \# -> #, 1
    # \\\.\+ -> ., 1 (or more, but let's say 1)
    # \\\.\{(\d+)\} -> ., group(1)
    # \\\. -> ., 1
    # \\\.\* -> ., 0 (or more)
    
    res = []
    # Replace #{N} with #N
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    # Replace \.{N} with .N
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    # Replace \.+ with .+
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    # Replace \.* with \.*
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    # Replace \. with .
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    
    # Now p is like .*#2.#1.#2.#2.#2.#1.#1.#1.#3.#1.#2.#2.#2.#3.#3.#1.#3
    # Let's parse this.
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

for i, p in enumerate(lookbehind_patterns):
    print(f"Block {i}: {parse_pattern(p)}")

    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*)",
]

def parse_pattern(p):
    p = p[1:-1]
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    
    res = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

for i, p in enumerate(lookbehind_patterns):
    print(f"Block {i}: {parse_pattern(p)}")

lookahead_pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?=(.*?)\)', re.DOTALL)
lookaheads = lookahead_pattern.findall(script_js, flags=re.DOTALL)

def get_segments(la):

# Let's try a simpler approach.
# For each lookahead, we find all (?:...) and their multipliers.
# Then we extract the A and type.

def parse_lookahead(la):
    # la is the content of (?=...)
    # We'll find all (?:...) and their multipliers.
    
    # A segment is (?:.{A}[.#].{B})
    # We'll use re.finditer to find all (?:...)
    
    # Let's find all (?:...) and then look for the multiplier.
    
    # We'll find all (?:...) using:
    # r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)'
    
    # BUT, the multiplier follows the )
    # So we need to find the pattern: (?:...) followed by [*+?]|{\d+}
    
    segments = []
    # We use finditer to find all (?:...) AND the multiplier
    # The pattern for the segment: \(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?
    segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?')
    for match in segment_regex.finditer(la):
        content = match.group('content')
        mult = match.group('mult') or ""
        # content is something like {91}\..{9} or {91}#.{9}
        # Let's find A and type.
        # It's always {A}[.#]
        m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}([\.#])', content)
        if m:
            A = int(m.group(1))
            t = m.group(2)
            segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        elif '{100}' in content and '\\.' in content: # for (?:.{100}\.)
             A = 100
             t = '.'
             segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        elif '{100}' in content and '#' in content: # for (?:.{100}#)
             A = 100
             t = '#'
             segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        elif '\\.' in content: # for (?:.{A}\.)
             m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
             if m:
                 A = int(m.group(1))
                 segments.append({'A': A, 'type': '.', 'mult': mult})
        elif '#' in content: # for (?:.{A}#)
             m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
             if m:
                 A = int(m.group(1))
                 segments.append({'A': A, 'type': '#', 'mult': mult})
    return segments

all_constraints = {} # A -> list of (type, mult)

# Let's use the new parse_lookahead.
def parse_lookahead_fixed(la):
    segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?')
    segments = []
    for match in segment_regex.finditer(la):
        content = match.group('content')
        mult = match.group('mult') or ""
        # content can be:
        # {91}\..{9}
        # {91}#.{9}
        # {100}\.
        # {100}#
        # {A}\.
        # {A}#
        m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}([\.#])', content)
        if m:
            A = int(m.group(1))
            t = m.group(2)
            segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        else:
            # Try the other forms
            m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
            if m:
                A = int(m.group(1))
                if '\\.' in content:
                    t = '.'
                elif '#' in content:
                    t = '#'
                else:
                    continue
                segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
    return segments

all_constraints = {} # A -> list of (type, mult)
for la in lookaheads:
    segs = parse_lookahead_fixed(la)
    for s in segs:
        A = s['A']
        if A not in all_constraints:
            all_constraints[A] = []
        all_constraints[A].append((s['type'], s['mult']))

# Now for each A, find the sequence of 19 chars that satisfies all its constraints.
def satisfies(seq, constraints):
    # seq is a string of 19 chars
    # constraints is a list of (type, mult)
    for t, mult in constraints:
        # Check if seq satisfies the pattern (t, mult)
        # if mult == '*': zero or more t
        # if mult == '+': one or more t
        # if mult == '{N}': exactly N t
        
        # Wait, the pattern is NOT just (t, mult).
        # The lookahead is a sequence of (t, mult) for the SAME A.
        # So seq must be (t1, mult1) followed by (t2, mult2) etc.
        # where the total length is 19.
        pass
    return True

# Let's rewrite this. For a given A, the lookahead is a sequence of (type, mult).
# We'll find the sequence of 19 characters.

def solve_A(A, constraints_for_A):
    # constraints_for_A is a list of (type, mult)
    # We want to find a sequence of 19 chars.
    # This is a simple DP or backtracking.
    
    memo = {}
    def backtrack(idx, constraint_idx):
        if constraint_idx == len(constraints_for_A):
            return idx == 19
        
        state = (idx, constraint_idx)
        if state in memo: return memo[state]
        
        t, mult = constraints_for_A[constraint_idx]
        
        # Try all possible number of characters 't' for this constraint
        # depending on the multiplier.
        
        # But wait, what if the next constraint is also for the same A?
        # Yes, it is.
        
        # If mult == '*': 0, 1, 2...
        # If mult == '+': 1, 2, 3...
        # If mult == '{N}': N
        
        # Wait, the segments in a lookahead are for the SAME A.
        # So the lookahead is: (t1, m1) then (t2, m2) ...
        # where the total number of characters is 19.
        
        # However, we also have the OTHER character (not t).
        # But in the lookahead, the OTHER character is ALWAYS '.'.
        # No, the lookahead is (?:.{A}#.{B}) or (?:.{A}\..{B}).
        # This means the character at index A is # or .
        # It doesn't say anything about the other characters in the block.
        # So the lookahead ONLY constrains the sequence S_A.
        # The character at index A is 't' or '.'? 
        # No, the lookahead is about the character at index A.
        # If the segment is (?:.{A}#.{B}), then the character at index A is '#'.
        # If the segment is (?:.{A}\..{B}), then the character at index A is '.'.
        
        # So the sequence S_A must be:
        # (t1, m1) then (t2, m2) then (t3, m3) ...
        # where t_i is '#' or '.' and m_i is the multiplier.
        # AND the characters in S_A MUST be the ones specified.
        # If the type is '#', we must have '#'. If it is '.', we must have '.'.
        # But there are NO other characters in the lookahead!
        # This means that for a given A, the lookahead is just a pattern for S_A.
        # For example: (type='.', mult='*'), (type='#', mult='4'), (type='.', mult='+')
        # means S_A is: some dots, then 4 hashes, then one or more dots.
        # The total length must be 19.
        
        # This is exactly what I need!
        pass
    return []

# Let's implement this.
def solve_A_final(constraints):
    # constraints is a list of (type, mult)
    # where type is '#' or '.'
    
    import itertools
    
    # We can use DP: dp[constraint_idx][current_length] = possible (boolean)
    # But we need to find the actual sequence.
    
    # Let's use a simple backtracking.
    def find_seq(c_idx, length):
        if c_idx == len(constraints):
            return "" if length == 19 else None
        
        t, mult = constraints[c_idx]
        
        # Determine possible counts for this constraint
        counts = []
        if mult == '*':
            counts = range(0, 20 - length)
        elif mult == '+':
            counts = range(1, 20 - length)
        elif mult.startswith('{'):
            n = int(mult[1:-1])
            if length + n <= 19:
                counts = [n]
        else:
            # No multiplier, means count is 1?
            # Let's check the regex. (?:...) is not a thing, it's (?:...)+ or (?:...)*
            # If there's no multiplier, it's exactly 1.
            counts = [1]
            
        for count in counts:
            res = find_seq(c_idx + 1, length + count)
            if res is not None:
                return t * count + res
        return None

    return find_seq(0, 0)

# Now we do this for each A.
# But wait, some A might have NO constraints!
# In that case, we'll use the lookbehinds.

# Let's collect all lookahead constraints.
# all_constraints[A] = list of (type, mult)
# Wait, my parse_lookahead_fixed already does this.

for A in range(101):
    if A in all_constraints:
        seq = solve_A_final(all_constraints[A])
        if seq:
            print(f"A={A}: {seq}")
        else:
            print(f"A={A}: No solution found")
    else:
        print(f"A={A}: No constraints")

{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{404})(?<!.{405})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{505})(?<!.{506})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{606})(?<!.{607})(\\.*#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{707})(?<!.{708})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{808})(?<!.{809})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{909})(?<!.{910})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{1010})(?<!.{1011})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1111})(?<!.{1112})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1212})(?<!.{1213})(\\.*#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{1313})(?<!.{1314})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1414})(?<!.{1415})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1515})(?<!.{1516})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1616})(?<!.{1617})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

lookahead_pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?=(.*?)\)', re.DOTALL)
lookaheads = lookahead_pattern.findall(script_js, flags=re.DOTALL)

def parse_lookahead_fixed(la):
    segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?')
    segments = []
    for match in segment_regex.finditer(la):
        content = match.group('content')
        mult = match.group('mult') or ""
        m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}([\.#])', content)
        if m:
            A = int(m.group(1))
            t = m.group(2)
            segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        else:
            m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
            if m:
                A = int(m.group(1))
                if '\\.' in content:
                    t = '.'
                elif '#' in content:
                    t = '#'
                else:
                    continue
                segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
    return segments

all_constraints = {}
for la in lookaheads:
    segs = parse_lookahead_fixed(la)
    if not segs: continue
    A = segs[0]['A']
    if A not in all_constraints:
        all_constraints[A] = []
    all_constraints[A].append(segs)

def solve_A_final(constraints):
    import itertools

    def find_seq(c_idx, length):
        if c_idx == len(constraints):
            return "" if length == 19 else None
        
        t, mult = constraints[c_idx]
        counts = []
        if mult == '*':
            counts = range(0, 20 - length)
        elif mult == '+':
            counts = range(1, 20 - length)
        elif mult.startswith('{'):
            n = int(mult[1:-1])
            if length + n <= 19:
                counts = [n]
        else:
            counts = [1]
            
        for count in counts:
            res = find_seq(c_idx + 1, length + count)
            if res is not None:
                return t * count + res
        return None

    return find_seq(0, 0)

grid = [['' for _ in range(101)] for _ in range(19)]
for A in range(101):
    if A in all_constraints:
        seq = solve_A_final(all_constraints[A])
        if seq:
            for k in range(19):
                grid[k][A] = seq[k]
        else:
            print(f"Error: A={A} no solution")
    else:
        # Use lookbehind to determine A
        # But wait, if A is not in lookaheads, we can't know its value
        # UNLESS the lookbehinds for each block determine it.
        # Let's check if all A's are in all_constraints.
        pass

# Let's check which A's are missing.
missing_A = [A for A in range(101) if A not in all_constraints]
print(f"Missing A: {missing_A}")

# For missing A, we'll use the lookbehind patterns.
# But the lookbehind pattern for block k is a string of (char, count).
# Let's parse the lookbehind patterns again.
# (see the previous part of the script)

# I will use the lookbehind patterns to fill in the grid for missing A's.
# For each block k, and for each missing A, we try to find the character at A.

# Let's do the parse_pattern again.
def parse_pattern_v2(p):
    p = p[1:-1]
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    
    res = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

# Re-run the loop with the missing A's.
# First, let's get the parsed patterns for all 19 blocks.
parsed_patterns = [parse_pattern_v2(p) for p in lookbehind_patterns]

# For each block k and missing A, we find the character.
# Since the pattern is (dots, pattern, dots), we can try all possible offsets.
# But wait, if the pattern is (dots, pattern, dots), then the pattern is a sequence of (char, count).
# The total number of characters is 101.
# The dots at the beginning (offset) and end (101 - pattern_length - offset) are unknown.

for k in range(19):
    pattern = parsed_patterns[k]
    pattern_len = sum(c for t, c in pattern)
    for A in missing_A:
        # Try all possible offsets
        found = False
        for offset in range(101 - pattern_len + 1):
            # Does this offset match A?
            # We need to check the pattern.
            # This is hard because the pattern is a sequence of (type, count).
            # We need to find which character is at index A.
            # The character at index A is at (A - offset) in the pattern.
            
            curr_idx = 0
            for t, c in pattern:
                if curr_idx <= (A - offset) < curr_idx + c:
                    grid[k][A] = t
                    found = True
                    break
                curr_idx += c
            if found: break
            
            # If offset is for the first \.*, it can be any number of dots.
            # But the pattern is fixed. The only things that are NOT fixed are the 
            # number of dots at the beginning and end.
            # So the # positions are at: offset + position_in_pattern.
            # This is exactly what I'm doing.
            
        if not found:
            # If not found, maybe A is one of the dots from \.* or \.+?
            # But wait, if A is not in any lookahead, it's likely a dot.
            grid[k][A] = '.'

# Now let's assemble the flag.
flag = ""
for k in range(19):
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            grid[k][A] = '.' # Default to dot
        flag += grid[k][A]

print(flag)

{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{404})(?<!.{405})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{505})(?<!.{506})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{606})(?<!.{607})(\\.*#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{707})(?<!.{708})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{808})(?<!.{809})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{909})(?<!.{910})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{1010})(?<!.{1011})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1111})(?<!.{1112})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1212})(?<!.{1213})(\\.*#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{1313})(?<!.{1314})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1414})(?<!.{1415})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1515})(?<!.{1516})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1616})(?<!.{1617})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

lookahead_pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?=(.*?)\)', re.DOTALL)
lookaheads = lookahead_pattern.findall(script_js, flags=re.DOTALL)

def parse_lookahead_fixed(la):
    segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?')
    segments = []
    for match in segment_regex.finditer(la):
        content = match.group('content')
        mult = match.group('mult') or ""
        m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}([\.#])', content)
        if m:
            A = int(m.group(1))
            t = m.group(2)
            segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        else:
            m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
            if m:
                A = int(m.group(1))
                if '\\.' in content:
                    t = '.'
                elif '#' in content:
                    t = '#'
                else:
                    continue
                segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
    return segments

all_constraints = {}
for la in lookaheads:
    segs = parse_lookahead_fixed(la)
    if not segs: continue
    A = segs[0]['A']
    if A not in all_constraints:
        all_constraints[A] = []
    all_constraints[A].append(segs)

def solve_A_final(constraints):
    def find_seq(c_idx, length):
        if c_idx == len(constraints):
            return "" if length == 19 else None
        t, mult = constraints[c_idx]
        counts = []
        if mult == '*':
            counts = range(0, 20 - length)
        elif mult == '+':
            counts = range(1, 20 - length)
        elif mult.startswith('{'):
            n = int(mult[1:-1])
            if length + n <= 19:
                counts = [n]
        else:
            counts = [1]
        for count in counts:
            res = find_seq(c_idx + 1, length + count)
            if res is not None:
                return t * count + res
        return None
    return find_seq(0, 0)

grid = [['' for _ in range(101)] for _ in range(19)]
for A in range(101):
    if A in all_constraints:
        seq = solve_A_final(all_constraints[A])
        if seq:
            for k in range(19):
                grid[k][A] = seq[k]

# Now use lookbehinds to fill in missing A's.
lookbehind_patterns = [
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*)",
]

def parse_pattern_v2(p):
    p = p[1:-1]
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    res = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

parsed_patterns = [parse_pattern_v2(p) for p in lookbehind_patterns]

for k in range(19):
    pattern = parsed_patterns[k]
    pattern_len = sum(c for t, c in pattern)
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            found = False
            for offset in range(101 - pattern_len + 1):
                curr_idx = 0
                match_offset = False
                for t, c in pattern:
                    if curr_idx <= (A - offset) < curr_idx + c:
                        if grid[k][A] == '' or grid[k][A] == t:
                            grid[k][A] = t
                            match_offset = True
                            break
                    curr_idx += c
                if match_offset:
                    found = True
                    break
            if not found:
                grid[k][A] = '.'

flag = ""
for k in range(19):
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            grid[k][A] = '.'
        flag += grid[k][A]
print(flag)

    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*)",
]

def parse_pattern_v2(p):
    p = p[1:-1]
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    res = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

parsed_patterns = [parse_pattern_v2(p) for p in lookbehind_patterns]

\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1616})(?<!.{1617})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

lookahead_pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?=(.*?)\)', re.DOTALL)
lookaheads = lookahead_pattern.findall(script_js, flags=re.DOTALL)

def parse_lookahead_fixed(la):
    segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?')
    segments = []
    for match in segment_regex.finditer(la):
        content = match.group('content')
        mult = match.group('mult') or ""
        m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}([\.#])', content)
        if m:
            A = int(m.group(1))
            t = m.group(2)
            segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        else:
            m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
            if m:
                A = int(m.group(1))
                if '\\.' in content:
                    t = '.'
                elif '#' in content:
                    t = '#'
                else:
                    continue
                segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
    return segments

all_constraints = {}
for la in lookaheads:
    segs = parse_lookahead_fixed(la)
    if not segs: continue
    A = segs[0]['A']
    if A not in all_constraints:
        all_constraints[A] = []
    all_constraints[A].append(segs)

def solve_A_final(constraints):
    def find_seq(c_idx, length):
        if c_idx == len(constraints):
            return "" if length == 19 else None
        t, mult = constraints[c_idx]
        counts = []
        if mult == '*':
            counts = range(0, 20 - length)
        elif mult == '+':
            counts = range(1, 20 - length)
        elif mult.startswith('{'):
            n = int(mult[1:-1])
            if length + n <= 19:
                counts = [n]
        else:
            counts = [1]
        for count in counts:
            res = find_seq(c_idx + 1, length + count)
            if res is not None:
                return t * count + res
        return None
    return find_seq(0, 0)

grid = [['' for _ in range(101)] for _ in range(19)]
for A in range(101):
    if A in all_constraints:
        seq = solve_A_final(all_constraints[A])
        if seq:
            for k in range(19):
                grid[k][A] = seq[k]

lookbehind_patterns = [
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*)",
]

def parse_pattern_v2(p):
    p = p[1:-1]
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    res = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

parsed_patterns = [parse_pattern_v2(p) for p in lookbehind_patterns]

for k in range(19):
    pattern = parsed_patterns[k]
    pattern_len = sum(c for t, c in pattern)
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            found = False
            for offset in range(101 - pattern_len + 1):
                curr_idx = 0
                match_offset = False
                for t, c in pattern:
                    if curr_idx <= (A - offset) < curr_idx + c:
                        if grid[k][A] == '' or grid[k][A] == t:
                            grid[k][A] = t
                            match_offset = True
                            break
                    curr_idx += c
                if match_offset:
                    found = True
                    break
            if not found:
                grid[k][A] = '.'

flag = ""
for k in range(19):
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            grid[k][A] = '.'
        flag += grid[k][A]
print(flag)
