{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{404})(?<!.{405})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{505})(?<!.{506})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{606})(?<!.{607})(\\.*#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{707})(?<!.{708})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{808})(?<!.{809})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{909})(?<!.{910})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{1010})(?<!.{1011})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1111})(?<!.{1112})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1212})(?<!.{1213})(\\.*#{3}\\.*)(?<=.{1313})(?<!.{1314})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1414})(?<!.{1415})(\\.*#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1515})(?<!.{1516})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1616})(?<!.{1617})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

lookahead_pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?=(.*?)\)', re.DOTALL)
lookaheads = lookahead_pattern.findall(script_js, flags=re.DOTALL)

def parse_lookahead_fixed(la):
    segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?')
    segments = []
    for match in segment_regex.finditer(la):
        content = match.group('content')
        mult = match.group('mult') or ""
        m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}([\.#])', content)
        if m:
            A = int(m.group(1))
            t = m.group(2)
            segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        else:
            m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
            if m:
                A = int(m.group(1))
                if '\\.' in content:
                    t = '.'
                elif '#' in content:
                    t = '#'
                else:
                    continue
                segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
    return segments

all_constraints = {}
for la in lookaheads:
    segs = parse_lookahead_fixed(la)
    if not segs: continue
    A = segs[0]['A']
    if A not in all_constraints:
        all_constraints[A] = []
    all_constraints[A].append(segs)

def solve_A_final(constraints):
    def find_seq(c_idx, length):
        if c_idx == len(constraints):
            return "" if length == 19 else None
        t, mult = constraints[c_idx]
        counts = []
        if mult == '*':
            counts = range(0, 20 - length)
        elif mult == '+':
            counts = range(1, 20 - length)
        elif mult.startswith('{'):
            n = int(mult[1:-1])
            if length + n <= 19:
                counts = [n]
        else:
            counts = [1]
        for count in counts:
            res = find_seq(c_idx + 1, length + count)
            if res is not None:
                return t * count + res
        return None
    return find_seq(0, 0)

grid = [['' for _ in range(101)] for _ in range(19)]
for A in range(101):
    if A in all_constraints:
        seq = solve_A_final(all_constraints[A])
        if seq:
            for k in range(19):
                grid[k][A] = seq[k]

lookbehind_patterns = [
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*)",
]

def parse_pattern_v2(p):
    p = p[1:-1]
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    res = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

parsed_patterns = [parse_pattern_v2(p) for p in lookbehind_patterns]

for k in range(19):
    pattern = parsed_patterns[k]
    pattern_len = sum(c for t, c in pattern)
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            found = False
            for offset in range(101 - pattern_len + 1):
                curr_idx = 0
                match_offset = False
                for t, c in pattern:
                    if curr_idx <= (A - offset) < curr_idx + c:
                        if grid[k][A] == '' or grid[k][A] == t:
                            grid[k][A] = t
                            match_offset = True
                            break
                    curr_idx += c
                if match_offset:
                    found = True
                    break
            if not found:
                grid[k][A] = '.'

flag = ""
for k in range(19):
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            grid[k][A] = '.'
        flag += grid[k][A]
print(flag)

\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.*)(?<=.{1616})(?<!.{1617})(\\.*#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1717})(?<!.{1718})(\\.*#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{3}\\.+#{3}\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#\\.+#{2}\\.*)(?<=.{1818})(?<!.{1819})(\\.*)(?<=.{1919})(?<!.{1920})$/;
"""

import re

lookahead_pattern = re.compile(r'\(\?=(.*?)\)', re.DOTALL)
lookaheads = lookahead_pattern.findall(script_js)

def parse_lookahead_fixed(la):
    segment_regex = re.compile(r'\(\?:\.(?P<content>.*?)\)(?P<mult>[*+?]|{\d+})?')
    segments = []
    for match in segment_regex.finditer(la):
        content = match.group('content')
        mult = match.group('mult') or ""
        m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}([\.#])', content)
        if m:
            A = int(m.group(1))
            t = m.group(2)
            segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
        else:
            m = re.search(r'\{(\d+)\}', content)
            if m:
                A = int(m.group(1))
                if '\\.' in content:
                    t = '.'
                elif '#' in content:
                    t = '#'
                else:
                    continue
                segments.append({'A': A, 'type': t, 'mult': mult})
    return segments

all_constraints = {}
for la in lookaheads:
    segs = parse_lookahead_fixed(la)
    if not segs: continue
    A = segs[0]['A']
    if A not in all_constraints:
        all_constraints[A] = []
    all_constraints[A].append(segs)

def solve_A_final(constraints):
    def find_seq(c_idx, length):
        if c_idx == len(constraints):
            return "" if length == 19 else None
        t, mult = constraints[c_idx]
        counts = []
        if mult == '*':
            counts = range(0, 20 - length)
        elif mult == '+':
            counts = range(1, 20 - length)
        elif mult.startswith('{'):
            n = int(mult[1:-1])
            if length + n <= 19:
                counts = [n]
        else:
            counts = [1]
        for count in counts:
            res = find_seq(c_idx + 1, length + count)
            if res is not None:
                return t * count + res
        return None
    return find_seq(0, 0)

grid = [['' for _ in range(101)] for _ in range(19)]
for A in range(101):
    if A in all_constraints:
        seq = solve_A_final(all_constraints[A])
        if seq:
            for k in range(19):
                grid[k][A] = seq[k]

lookbehind_patterns = [
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.*)",
    r"(\.*#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#{3}\.+#{3}\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#\.+#{2}\.*)",
    r"(\.*)",
]

def parse_pattern_v2(p):
    p = p[1:-1]
    p = re.sub(r'#\{(\d+)\}', r'#\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\{(\d+)\}', r'.\1', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\+', r'.+', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.\*', r'.*', p)
    p = re.sub(r'\\\.', r'.', p)
    res = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(p):
        if p[i] == '.':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '*':
                res.append(('.', 0))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1] == '+':
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 2
            elif i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('.', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('.', 1))
                i += 1
        elif p[i] == '#':
            if i+1 < len(p) and p[i+1].isdigit():
                j = i+1
                while j < len(p) and p[j].isdigit():
                    j += 1
                res.append(('#', int(p[i+1:j])))
                i = j
            else:
                res.append(('#', 1))
                i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return res

parsed_patterns = [parse_pattern_v2(p) for p in lookbehind_patterns]

for k in range(19):
    pattern = parsed_patterns[k]
    pattern_len = sum(c for t, c in pattern)
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            found = False
            for offset in range(101 - pattern_len + 1):
                curr_idx = 0
                match_offset = False
                for t, c in pattern:
                    if curr_idx <= (A - offset) < curr_idx + c:
                        if grid[k][A] == '' or grid[k][A] == t:
                            grid[k][A] = t
                            match_offset = True
                            break
                    curr_idx += c
                if match_offset:
                    found = True
                    break
            if not found:
                grid[k][A] = '.'

flag = ""
for k in range(19):
    for A in range(101):
        if grid[k][A] == '':
            grid[k][A] = '.'
        flag += grid[k][A]
print(flag)

```bash
$ [python] import re

with open('script.js', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

the_flag_match = re.search(r'const theFlag = /(.*?)/;', content)
the_flag_regex = the_flag_match.group(1)

# Parse lookaheads
lookaheads = []
i = 0
while i < len(the_flag_regex):
    if the_flag_regex[i:i+3] == "(?=":
        start = i
        count = 1
        for j in range(i+3, len(the_flag_regex)):
            if the_flag_regex[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or the_flag_regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                count += 1
            elif the_flag_regex[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or the_flag_regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                count -= 1
                if count == 0:
                    lookaheads.append(the_flag_regex[start+2:j])
                    i = j
                    break
        else:
            break
    i += 1

# For each lookahead, extract index and the 19-char regex
# Example: (?:.{91}\..{9})*(?:.{91}#.{9}){4}(?:.{91}\..{9})+(?:.{91}#.{9}){5}(?:.{91}\..{9})*
# We want to find A in .{A} and then the pattern.

rules = {} # index -> list of regexes

for lh in lookaheads:
    # A lookahead might have multiple parts like (?:A\..B)* (?:A#..B){4} ...
    # Let's find all the (?:...) parts.
    parts = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(lh):
        if lh[i:i+3] == "(?:":
            start = i
            count = 1
            for j in range(i+3, len(lh)):
                if lh[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count += 1
                elif lh[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count -= 1
                    if count == 0:
                        parts.append(lh[start+3:j])
                        i = j
                        break
            else:
                break
        i += 1
    
    # Now 'parts' contains things like '.{91}\..{9}' and '.{91}#.{9}'
    # We need to identify the index A and whether it's '.' or '#'
    
    # First, let's find the index A from the first part.
    if not parts:
        continue
    
    # Let's use a regex to find the index A and the type in each part.
    # The pattern is .{A}\..{B} or .{A}#.{B}
    
    # Actually, all parts in a lookahead should have the same A.
    # Let's check.
    
    # Let's find the index A from the first part.
    match_a = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}', parts[0])
    if not match_a:
        # Maybe it's a different format like (?:\..{100})
        # If it's (?:\..{100}), then index A=0 and type is '.'
        if re.search(r'\.\.{100}', parts[0]):
            A = 0
            type_part = '.'
        else:
            continue
    else:
        A = int(match_a.group('A'))
        if '\\.' in parts[0]:
            type_part = '.'
        elif '#' in parts[0]:
            type_part = '#'
        elif '.' in parts[0] and '\\.' not in parts[0]: # Case like (?:\..{100})
             # If it's \..{100}, it's a dot followed by 100 chars. Index is 0.
             # But the regex is \..{100}, which is literal dot + 100 chars.
             # This corresponds to index 0.
             A = 0
             type_part = '.'
        else:
             # Let's re-examine the types.
             # If the part is \..{100}, it's a literal dot.
             # If the part is .#.{99}, it's a literal hash.
             # Wait, the regex is (?:.{A}\..{B})
             # Let's just check for \. or #
             if '\\.' in parts[0]:
                 type_part = '.'
             elif '#' in parts[0]:
                 type_part = '#'
             elif parts[0].startswith('\\.'):
                 type_part = '.'
             else:
                 type_part = None
    
    if A is None or type_part is None:
        # Try another way
        # Let's just look at the whole LH and find the first index A and the type.
        # Wait, I'll just find the first A in the LH.
        match_a_all = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}', lh)
        if match_a_all:
            A = int(match_a_all.group('A'))
            # Now check if the first part is \. or #
            # This is hard. Let's just look at parts[0]
            if '\\.' in parts[0] or parts[0].startswith('\\.'):
                type_part = '.'
            elif '#' in parts[0]:
                type_part = '#'
            else:
                type_part = None
        else:
            # Check for the \..{100} case
            if '\\.' in lh:
                A = 0
                type_part = '.'
            else:
                continue

    if A is not None and type_part is not None:
        # Now build the 19-char regex.
        # We need to replace each part with its type and keep the repetition.
        # But the parts in 'parts' are just the content of the (?:...)
        # We need the repetitions too.
        
        # Let's re-parse the LH to get the parts with their quantifiers.
        # Example: (?:.{91}\..{9})*(?:.{91}#.{9}){4}...
        
        # Let's use a different approach. 
        # The lookahead LH is a string like: (?:A\..B)*(?:A#..B){4}...
        # We want to transform it to: (\.)*(#){4}(\.)+...
        
        # We'll find all (?:...) with their quantifiers.
        # A quantifier can be *, +, {N}, {N,M}, or nothing.
        
        pattern_on_19 = ""
        i = 0
        while i < len(lh):
            if lh[i:i+3] == "(?:":
                start = i
                count = 1
                for j in range(i+3, len(lh)):
                    if lh[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                        count += 1
                    elif lh[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                        count -= 1
                        if count == 0:
                            # Found the (?:...) part
                            part_content = lh[start+3:j]
                            # Now find the quantifier after ')'
                            quantifier = ""
                            k = j + 1
                            if k < len(lh) and lh[k] in '*+?':
                                quantifier = lh[k]
                                i = k + 1
                            elif k < len(lh) and lh[k] == '{':
                                end_q = lh.find('}', k)
                                quantifier = lh[k:end_q+1]
                                i = end_q + 1
                            else:
                                i = j + 1
                            
                            # Determine the type from part_content
                            if '\\.' in part_content or part_content.startswith('\\.'):
                                type_char = r'\.'
                            elif '#' in part_content:
                                type_char = '#'
                            else:
                                # This must be the \..{100} case
                                type_char = r'\.'
                                
                            pattern_on_19 += f"{type_char}{quantifier}"
                            break
                else:
                    break
            else:
                i += 1
        
        if pattern_on_19:
            if A not in rules:
                rules[A] = []
            rules[A].append(pattern_on_19)

for a in sorted(rules.keys()):
    print(f"Index {a}: {rules[a]}")

print(f"Total indices covered: {len(rules)}")

```

```bash
$ [python] import re

with open('script.js', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

the_flag_match = re.search(r'const theFlag = /(.*?)/;', content)
the_flag_regex = the_flag_match.group(1)

# Let's find all (?:...) in the regex
# We'll use a stack-based approach to find all (?:...) blocks
def find_non_capturing_groups(regex):
    groups = []
    stack = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(regex):
        if regex[i:i+3] == "(?:":
            stack.append(i)
            i += 3
        elif regex[i] == ")" and stack:
            start = stack.pop()
            groups.append((start, i))
            i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    return groups

groups = find_non_capturing_groups(the_flag_regex)

# Now, for each group, check if it's a constraint on an index
# A constraint group looks like (?:.{A}\..{B}) or (?:.{A}#.{B})
# and it may have a quantifier after it.

constraints = [] # list of (index, type, quantifier)

for start, end in groups:
    group_content = the_flag_regex[start+3:end]
    
    # Check if it matches the pattern
    # Pattern 1: .{A}\..{B}
    # Pattern 2: .{A}#.{B}
    
    match = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}\.(?P<type>[.#]).{(?P<B>\d+)}', group_content)
    # Wait, the pattern in the regex is \..{B} or #.{B}
    # Let's use a more robust regex to match the content.
    # The content is something like .{A}\..{B} or .{A}#.{B}
    
    # Let's re-examine the content:
    # (?:.{91}\..{9})
    # The content is .{91}\..{9}
    # The dot is escaped in the regex as \.
    
    # Let's try to find the A and type
    # Looking at the content:
    # If it has '\.', type is '.'
    # If it has '#', type is '#'
    # If it has '.', type is '.' (but we must be careful about the escape)
    
    A = None
    type_char = None
    
    # Try to find A
    a_match = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}', group_content)
    if a_match:
        A = int(a_match.group('A'))
        if '\\.' in group_content or group_content.startswith('\\.'):
            type_char = '.'
        elif '#' in group_content:
            type_char = '#'
        elif '.' in group_content:
             # This might be the \..{B} case where the dot is not escaped in the group_content
             # but it was in the regex. Actually, it is escaped.
             type_char = '.'
        else:
             # Check if it's the \..{100} case
             if '\\.' in group_content:
                 type_char = '.'
    
    if A is not None and type_char is not None:
        # Now find the quantifier in the original regex after the group
        # The group ends at 'end'. The quantifier is at the_flag_regex[end:something]
        
        quantifier = ""
        k = end
        if k < len(the_flag_regex):
            if the_flag_regex[k] in '*+?':
                quantifier = the_flag_regex[k]
            elif the_flag_regex[k] == '{':
                end_q = the_flag_regex.find('}', k)
                if end_q != -1:
                    quantifier = the_flag_regex[k:end_q+1]
        
        constraints.append((A, type_char, quantifier))

# Let's see what we found
from collections import defaultdict
idx_to_constraints = defaultdict(list)
for A, t, q in constraints:
    idx_to_constraints[A].append((t, q))

for a in sorted(idx_to_constraints.keys()):
    print(f"Index {a}: {idx_to_constraints[a]}")

print(f"Total constraints: {len(constraints)}")
print(f"Total indices: {len(idx_to_constraints)}")

def get_all_constraints(regex):
    constraints = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(regex):
        if regex[i:i+3] == "(?:":
            start = i
            count = 1
            for j in range(i+3, len(regex)):
                if regex[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count += 1
                elif regex[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count -= 1
                    if count == 0:
                        group_content = regex[start+3:j]
                        # Find A and type
                        a_match = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}', group_content)
                        if a_match:
                            A = int(a_match.group('A'))
                            # If it contains \. or doesn't contain #, it's a dot
                            if '\\.' in group_content or '#' not in group_content:
                                type_char = '.'
                            else:
                                type_char = '#'
                        else:
                            if '\\.' in group_content:
                                A = 0
                                type_char = '.'
                            else:
                                A = None
                        
                        if A is not None:
                            quantifier = ""
                            k = j + 1
                            if k < len(regex):
                                if regex[k] in '*+?':
                                    quantifier = regex[k]
                                elif regex[k] == '{':
                                    end_q = regex.find('}', k)
                                    if end_q != -1:
                                        quantifier = regex[k:end_q+1]
                            pattern = (r'\.' if type_char == '.' else '#') + quantifier
                            constraints.append((A, pattern))
                        i = j
                        break
            else:
                break
        i += 1
    return constraints

all_constraints = get_all_constraints(the_flag_regex)
idx_to_patterns = defaultdict(list)
for A, pattern in all_constraints:
    idx_to_patterns[A].append(pattern)

print(f"Total constraints: {len(all_constraints)}")
print(f"Total indices: {len(idx_to_patterns)}")

# Now find S_j for each column.
# Since 2^19 is small, we can pre-generate all 2^19 strings of '.' and '#'.
# Wait, 2^19 = 524288. This is very manageable.

# To speed up, for each column, we only check patterns that apply to it.
# Let's generate all 524288 strings.
# A string can be represented by an integer from 0 to 2^19 - 1.

def get_string(n):
    # n is an integer, bit i is 1 if char i is '#'
    return "".join(['#' if (n & (1 << i)) else '.' for i in range(19)])

# Actually, it's easier to use the bit position.
# But the regexes are on the whole 19-character string.
# Let's use strings.

valid_columns = {}
for a in range(101):
    patterns = idx_to_patterns.get(a, [])
    if not patterns:
        # If no constraints, all 2^19 are potentially valid.
        # But wait, if no constraints, we'll have to rely on row constraints.
        # For now, let's assume all are valid.
        valid_columns[a] = None
        continue
    
    # Pre-compile patterns
    compiled_patterns = [re.compile(p) for p in patterns]
    
    # Find all 19-char strings that match all patterns
    column_matches = []
    # To be faster, let's iterate over all 2^19 and check.
    for n in range(1 << 19):
        s = "".join(['#' if (n & (1 << i)) else '.' for i in range(19)])
        if all(p.match(s) for p in compiled_patterns):
            column_matches.append(s)
    
    if not column_matches:
        print(f"No valid string for column {a}")
    valid_columns[a] = column_matches

print("Finished finding valid columns.")

# Now we have valid_columns[a] which is a list of possible 19-char strings.
# We need to pick one for each 'a' such that each row satisfies the row regex.

# Let's also get the row regexes.
# The segments are at index 101*i.
# The segment regex is: (?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})(\.*PATTERN\.*)

row_regexes = []
# Wait, the segments are at 101, 202, ..., 1818.
# There are 18 segments (for blocks 1 to 18).
# And block 0 is (\.*).
# So there are 19 rows.
# Let's extract the segment patterns.

def get_row_regexes(regex):
    row_regexes = []
    # Block 0: (\.*)
    row_regexes.append(r'\.*')
    
    # Segments: (?<=.{101*i})(?<!.{101*i+1})(\.*PATTERN\.*)
    # We can find these by looking for (?<=.{N})(?<!.{N+1})(\.*PATTERN\.*)
    # The N is 101, 202, ...
    i = 0
    while i < len(regex):
        if regex[i:i+5] == "(?<=.{":
            # Find the N
            match_n = re.search(r'\.(?P<N>\d+)\)', regex[i:])
            if match_n:
                N = int(match_n.group('N'))
                # Now find the (?<!.{N+1})
                start_next = regex.find('(?<!.{', i)
                if start_next != -1 and start_next < regex.find('(', i+5): # Not quite right
                    # Let's just look for the segment pattern
                    pass
            i += 1
        else:
            i += 1
    # Let's use the segments we already found.
    return

$ [python] import re
from collections import defaultdict

with open('script.js', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

the_flag_match = re.search(r'const theFlag = /(.*?)/;', content)
the_flag_regex = the_flag_match.group(1)

def get_all_lookahead_regexes(regex):
    lookahead_regexes = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(regex):
        if regex[i:i+3] == "(?=":
            start = i
            count = 1
            for j in range(i+3, len(regex)):
                if regex[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count += 1
                elif regex[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count -= 1
                    if count == 0:
                        lookahead_regexes.append(regex[start+2:j])
                        i = j
                        break
            else:
                break
        i += 1
    return lookahead_regexes

lookahead_regexes = get_all_lookahead_regexes(the_flag_regex)

idx_to_regexes = defaultdict(list)

for lh in lookahead_regexes:
    # Find A
    a_match = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}', lh)
    if a_match:
        A = int(a_match.group('A'))
    else:
        # Check for \..{100}
        if '\\.' in lh:
            A = 0
        else:
            continue
            
    # Transform the lookahead regex into a 19-char regex
    # We'll replace all (?:.{A}\..{B}) with \. and (?:.{A}#.{B}) with #
    # and keep the quantifiers.
    
    # Let's use a more robust way to parse the lookahead.
    # The lookahead is composed of (?:...) blocks.
    
    lh_pattern = ""
    i = 0
    while i < len(lh):
        if lh[i:i+3] == "(?:":
            start = i
            count = 1
            for j in range(i+3, len(lh)):
                if lh[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count += 1
                elif lh[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count -= 1
                    if count == 0:
                        group_content = lh[start+3:j]
                        # Determine type
                        if '\\.' in group_content or '#' not in group_content:
                            type_char = r'\.'
                        else:
                            type_char = '#'
                        
                        # Quantifier
                        quantifier = ""
                        k = j + 1
                        if k < len(lh):
                            if lh[k] in '*+?':
                                quantifier = lh[k]
                            elif lh[k] == '{':
                                end_q = lh.find('}', k)
                                if end_q != -1:
                                    quantifier = lh[k:end_q+1]
                        
                        lh_pattern += type_char + quantifier
                        i = j
                        break
            else:
                break
        else:
            i += 1
            
    if lh_pattern:
        idx_to_regexes[A].append(lh_pattern)

# Check if any index has no regexes
for a in range(101):
    if a not in idx_to_regexes:
        pass # We'll handle this later

print(f"Found {len(idx_to_regexes)} indices with constraints.")
for a in sorted(idx_to_regexes.keys()):
    print(f"Index {a}: {idx_to_regexes[a]}")

$ [python] import re

with open('script.js', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

the_flag_match = re.search(r'const theFlag = /(.*?)/;', content)
the_flag_regex = the_flag_match.group(1)

def find_all_lookaheads(regex):
    results = []
    def backtrack(start_idx):
        i = start_idx
        while i < len(regex):
            if regex[i:i+3] == "(?=":
                # Find the matching closing parenthesis
                count = 1
                j = i + 3
                while j < len(regex) and count > 0:
                    if regex[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                        count += 1
                    elif regex[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                        count -= 1
                    j += 1
                if count == 0:
                    # Found a lookahead
                    lookahead_content = regex[i+3:j-1]
                    results.append((i, lookahead_content))
                    # Also look for nested ones
                    backtrack(i + 3)
                    i = j
                else:
                    i += 1
            else:
                i += 1
    backtrack(0)
    return results

all_lookaheads = find_all_lookaheads(the_flag_regex)
print(f"Found {len(all_lookaheads)} lookaheads")

# Now let's re-parse the constraints.
from collections import defaultdict
idx_to_regexes = defaultdict(list)

for _, lh in all_lookaheads:
    # A lookahead is a single regex for some index A.
    # We need to find A and transform the whole lh into a 19-char regex.
    
    # Let's find A
    a_match = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}', lh)
    if a_match:
        A = int(a_match.group('A'))
    else:
        if '\\.' in lh:
            A = 0
        else:
            continue

    # Transform lh into a 19-char regex.
    # Instead of parsing (?:...), let's just replace all .{A}\..{B} and .{A}#.{B}
    # with \. and #.
    
    # First, let's identify the pattern within the lookahead.
    # A lookahead is a sequence of (?:...) parts.
    # We can use regex to find all (?:...) parts and their quantifiers.
    
    lh_pattern = ""
    # We need to be careful with the (?:...) parsing.
    # Let's use the same logic as before.
    i = 0
    while i < len(lh):
        if lh[i:i+3] == "(?:":
            start = i
            count = 1
            for j in range(i+3, len(lh)):
                if lh[j] == '(' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count += 1
                elif lh[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or lh[j-1] != '\\'):
                    count -= 1
                    if count == 0:
                        group_content = lh[start+3:j]
                        if '\\.' in group_content or '#' not in group_content:
                            type_char = r'\.'
                        else:
                            type_char = '#'
                        
                        quantifier = ""
                        k = j + 1
                        if k < len(lh):
                            if lh[k] in '*+?':
                                quantifier = lh[k]
                            elif lh[k] == '{':
                                end_q = lh.find('}', k)
                                if end_q != -1:
                                    quantifier = lh[k:end_q+1]
                        
                        lh_pattern += type_char + quantifier
                        i = j
                        break
            else:
                break
        else:
            i += 1
    
    if lh_pattern:
        idx_to_regexes[A].append(lh_pattern)

for a in sorted(idx_to_regexes.keys()):
    print(f"Index {a}: {idx_to_regexes[a]}")

                count += 1
            elif the_flag_regex[j] == ')' and (j == 0 or the_flag_regex[j-1] != '\\'):
                count -= 1
                if count == 0:
                    group_content = the_flag_regex[start+3:j]
                    
                    # Check if it's a pattern like .{A}\..{B} or .{A}#.{B}
                    # We'll use a regex to match the group_content
                    # Pattern 1: .{A}\..{B}
                    # Pattern 2: .{A}#.{B}
                    
                    # Let's find A
                    a_match = re.search(r'\.{(?P<A>\d+)}', group_content)
                    if a_match:
                        A = int(a_match.group('A'))
                        # Determine type
                        if '\\.' in group_content or '#' not in group_content:
                            type_char = r'\.'
                        else:
                            type_char = '#'
                        
                        # Find quantifier
                        quantifier = ""
                        k = j + 1
                        if k < len(the_flag_regex):
                            if the_flag_regex[k] in '*+?':
                                quantifier = the_flag_regex[k]
                            elif the_flag_regex[k] == '{':
                                end_q = the_flag_regex.find('}', k)
                                if end_q != -1:
                                    quantifier = the_flag_regex[k:end_q+1]
                        
                        idx_to_patterns[A].append(type_char + quantifier)
                    
                    i = j
                    break
            else:
                break
        else:
            break
    else:
        i += 1

# Now, for each index, we have a list of pieces. 
# But we must be careful: if several pieces belong to the same lookahead,
# they must be in the correct order.
# My current approach of iterating through the whole regex and collecting 
# pieces for each index should naturally preserve the order.
# However, we need to make sure that all pieces for a given index A 
# come from the same lookahead or are consistent.
# Actually, if they are from different lookaheads, it's fine, they still 
# contribute to the same 19-character string.

# Wait, if I have two different lookaheads for the same index A,
# I should probably concatenate their regexes? No, they should be 
# ANDed. In regex, `(?=regex1)(?=regex2)` is the same as `regex1` AND `regex2`.
# My `idx_to_patterns[A]` is a list of regexes. I should treat them as ANDed.

print(f"Total indices: {len(idx_to_patterns)}")
for a in sorted(idx_to_patterns.keys()):
    print(f"Index {a}: {''.join(idx_to_patterns[a])}")

```
